### **A.4 Retarded Position and History Buffer**

Retarded time:

τ(r)=αrc_τ_(_r_)=_cαr_​

Retarded BH position via linear interpolation between buffer entries (t0,r0)(_t_0​,**r**0​) and (t1,r1)(_t_1​,**r**1​):

rBHret=r0+(t−τ)−t0t1−t0(r1−r0)**r**_BHret_​=**r**0​+_t_1​−_t_0​(_t_−_τ_)−_t_0​​(**r**1​−**r**0​)

**LaTeX:**

rBHret(t,r)=r0+t−τ−t0t1−t0(r1−r0),τ=αrc**r**_BHret_​(_t_,_r_)=**r**0​+_t_1​−_t_0​_t_−_τ_−_t_0​​(**r**1​−**r**0​),_τ_=_cαr_​

**Mathematica:**

```
tau[r_] := alpha*r/c
rBHret[t_, r_] := Module[{tTarget = t - tau[r], idx},
    idx = LengthWhile[bhTimes, # <= tTarget &];
    If[idx == 0, bhPositions[[1]],
       If[idx >= Length[bhTimes], Last[bhPositions],
          bhPositions[[idx]] + (tTarget - bhTimes[[idx]])/
          (bhTimes[[idx+1]] - bhTimes[[idx]]) *
          (bhPositions[[idx+1]] - bhPositions[[idx]])
    ]]
]
```

**Python:**

```
def retarded_position(bh_times, bh_positions, t_now, r, alpha, c):
    tau = alpha * r / c
    t_target = t_now - tau
    idx = np.searchsorted(bh_times, t_target, side='right')
    if idx == 0:
        return bh_positions[0]
    if idx >= len(bh_times):
        return bh_positions[-1]
    t0, t1 = bh_times[idx-1], bh_times[idx]
    r0, r1 = bh_positions[idx-1], bh_positions[idx]
    frac = (t_target - t0) / (t1 - t0)
    return r0 + frac * (r1 - r0)
```

### **A.5 Yahalom Retarded Acceleration**

From Yahalom (2013), the leading retardation correction to the gravitational acceleration in the weak-field, slow-motion limit is:

ar=Gc2[M¨2R^+M˙RR˙+MRR¨]**a**_r_​=_c_2_G_​[2_M_¨​_R_^+_RM_˙​**R**˙+_RM_​**R**¨]

In the simulator, the dominant term is retained:

ar≈αGM¨BH2c2R^**a**_r_​≈2_c_2_αGM_¨_BH_​​_R_^

The α_α_ parameter absorbs theoretical uncertainties and allows the strength of the retardation effect to be varied continuously.

**Physical interpretation of the 1/r01/**_**r**_**0 dependence:**

The absence of distance dependence in the leading retardation term is analogous to the radiation field of an accelerating electromagnetic source. In electromagnetism, the Liénard-Wiechert fields decompose into a velocity field (∝ 1/r21/_r_2, near-zone) and an acceleration field (∝ 1/r1/_r_, radiation zone). For gravitation, the analogous decomposition yields a retardation term that falls more slowly than the Newtonian 1/r21/_r_2 — becoming relatively more important at galactic scales. This is precisely the regime where dark matter is invoked observationally.

**LaTeX — full Yahalom correction:**

ar=Gc2(M¨2R^+M˙RR˙+MRR¨)**a**_r_​=_c_2_G_​(2_M_¨​_R_^+_RM_˙​**R**˙+_RM_​**R**¨)

**Mathematica:**

```
ar[t_] := G/c^2 * (MBHdd[t]/2 * Rhat[t]
                 + MBHd[t]/R[t] * Rdot[t]
                 + MBH[t]/R[t] * Rddot[t])
```

**Python:**

```
def yahalom_correction(G, c, M, Mdot, Mdd, R, R_hat, R_dot, R_ddot):
    return (G / c**2) * (
        Mdd / 2 * R_hat +
        Mdot / R * R_dot +
        M / R * R_ddot
    )
```

### **A.6 Kerr Metric and Frame-Dragging**

The Kerr metric in Boyer-Lindquist coordinates (t,r,θ,ϕ)(_t_,_r_,_θ_,_ϕ_):

ds2=−(1−rsrΣ)c2dt2−2rsrasin⁡2θΣc,dt,dϕ+ΣΔdr2+Σ,dθ2+(r2+a2+rsra2sin⁡2θΣ)sin⁡2θ,dϕ2_ds_2=−(1−Σ_rs_​_r_​)_c_2_dt_2−Σ2_rs_​_ra_sin2_θ_​_c_,_dt_,_dϕ_+ΔΣ​_dr_2+Σ,_dθ_2+(_r_2+_a_2+Σ_rs_​_ra_2sin2_θ_​)sin2_θ_,_dϕ_2

where:

Σ=r2+a2cos⁡2θ,Δ=r2−rsr+a2,rs=2GMc2,a=JMcΣ=_r_2+_a_2cos2_θ_,Δ=_r_2−_rs_​_r_+_a_2,_rs_​=_c_22_GM_​,_a_=_McJ_​

The frame-dragging angular velocity (Lense-Thirring precession rate):

ΩLT(r)=2GJc2r3=rsac2r3Ω_LT_​(_r_)=_c_2_r_32_GJ_​=2_r_3_rs_​_ac_​

In the simulator, the Lense-Thirring acceleration is implemented in the weak-field approximation:

aLT=2GJc2r3(v×r^)**a**_LT_​=_c_2_r_32_GJ_​(**v**×_r_^)

**LaTeX — Kerr metric:**

ds2=−(1−rsrΣ)c2dt2+ΣΔdr2+Σ,dθ2+(r2+a2+rsra2sin⁡2θΣ)sin⁡2θ,dϕ2−2rsrasin⁡2θΣc,dt,dϕ_ds_2=−(1−Σ_rs_​_r_​)_c_2_dt_2+ΔΣ​_dr_2+Σ,_dθ_2+(_r_2+_a_2+Σ_rs_​_ra_2sin2_θ_​)sin2_θ_,_dϕ_2−Σ2_rs_​_ra_sin2_θ_​_c_,_dt_,_dϕ_

**Mathematica:**

```
rs = 2*G*M/c^2;
a  = J/(M*c);
Sigma[r_,th_] := r^2 + a^2*Cos[th]^2
Delta[r_]     := r^2 - rs*r + a^2
OmegaLT[r_]   := rs*a*c/(2*r^3)
aLT[r_,v_]    := 2*G*J/(c^2*r^3) * Cross[v, rhat]
```

**Python:**

```
def frame_dragging_acc(J, r_vec, v_vec, G, c):
    r = np.linalg.norm(r_vec)
    r_hat = r_vec / r
    return (2*G*J / (c**2 * r**3)) * np.cross(v_vec, r_hat)
```

### **A.7 Schwinger Pair Production Rate**

The Schwinger pair production rate per unit volume per unit time in a uniform electric field E_E_ (Schwinger, 1951):

ΓE=αemE2π2∑n=1∞(−1)n+1n2exp⁡!(−nπEcE)Γ_E_​=_π_2_αem_​_E_2​_n_=1∑∞​_n_2(−1)_n_+1​exp!(−_EnπEc_​​)

where Ec=me2c3/(eℏ)_Ec_​=_me_2​_c_3/(_e_ℏ) is the critical (Schwinger) field. The leading term:

ΓE≈αemE2π2exp⁡!(−πEcE)Γ_E_​≈_π_2_αem_​_E_2​exp!(−_EπEc_​​)

For the magnetic analog, E→B_E_→_B_ (in Gaussian units where E_E_ and B_B_ have the same dimensions):

ΓB≈αemB2π2exp⁡!(−πBcB),Bc=me2c3eℏ≈4.414×1013,GΓ_B_​≈_π_2_αem_​_B_2​exp!(−_BπBc_​​),_Bc_​=_e_ℏ_me_2​_c_3​≈4.414×1013,G

**LaTeX:**

Γ≈αemB2π2exp⁡!(−πBcB),Bc=me2c3eℏΓ≈_π_2_αem_​_B_2​exp!(−_BπBc_​​),_Bc_​=_e_ℏ_me_2​_c_3​

**Mathematica:**

```
alphaEM = 1/137;
Bc = (me^2 * c^3)/(e * hbar);
GammaSch[B_] := (alphaEM * B^2 / Pi^2) * Exp[-Pi*Bc/B]
```

**Python:**

```
from scipy.constants import m_e, c, e, hbar, alpha
import numpy as np

Bc = m_e**2 * c**3 / (e * hbar)   # Schwinger field in SI (Tesla)

def schwinger_rate(B):
    """Pair production rate (leading term). B in Tesla."""
    return (alpha * B**2 / np.pi**2) * np.exp(-np.pi * Bc / B)
```

### **A.8 Boris Integrator (Complete)**

The Boris push for a relativistic charged particle in combined electric and magnetic fields (E,B)(**E**,**B**) over timestep ΔtΔ_t_:

**Step 1 — half electric push:**

u−=un+qEmΔt2,γ−=1+∣u−∣2/c2**u**−=**u**_n_​+_mq_**E**​2Δ_t_​,_γ_−=1+∣**u**−∣2/_c_2​

**Step 2 — magnetic rotation:**

t=qBmγ−Δt2,s=2t1+∣t∣2**t**=_mγ_−_q_**B**​2Δ_t_​,**s**=1+∣**t**∣22**t**​

u′=u−+u−×t,u+=u−+u′×s**u**′=**u**−+**u**−×**t**,**u**+=**u**−+**u**′×**s**

**Step 3 — half electric push:**

un+1=u++qEmΔt2,γn+1=1+∣un+1∣2/c2**u**_n_+1​=**u**++_mq_**E**​2Δ_t_​,_γn_+1​=1+∣**u**_n_+1​∣2/_c_2​

**Step 4 — position update:**

xn+1=xn+un+1γn+1Δt**x**_n_+1​=**x**_n_​+_γn_+1​**u**_n_+1​​Δ_t_

where u=γv**u**=_γ_**v** is the relativistic momentum per unit mass.

**LaTeX:**

u−=un+qEmΔt2,t=qBΔt2mγ−,s=2t1+t2**u**−=**u**_n_​+_mq_**E**​2Δ_t_​,**t**=2_mγ_−_q_**B**Δ_t_​,**s**=1+_t_22**t**​

u+=u−+(u−+u−×t)×s,un+1=u++qEmΔt2**u**+=**u**−+(**u**−+**u**−×**t**)×**s**,**u**_n_+1​=**u**++_mq_**E**​2Δ_t_​

**Mathematica:**

```
BorisStep[u_, E_, B_, q_, m_, dt_] := Module[
    {uMinus, gamma, t, s, uPrime, uPlus, uNew},
    uMinus = u + (q*E/m)*(dt/2);
    gamma  = Sqrt[1 + uMinus.uMinus/c^2];
    t      = q*B*dt/(2*m*gamma);
    s      = 2*t/(1 + t.t);
    uPrime = uMinus + Cross[uMinus, t];
    uPlus  = uMinus + Cross[uPrime, s];
    uNew   = uPlus + (q*E/m)*(dt/2);
    uNew
]
```

**Python:**

```
import numpy as np

def boris_push(x, u, q, m, E, B, dt, c):
    """
    Full relativistic Boris push.
    u = gamma * v (relativistic momentum per unit mass)
    """
    # Half electric push
    u_minus = u + (q * E / m) * (dt / 2)
    gamma_minus = np.sqrt(1 + np.dot(u_minus, u_minus) / c**2)
    
    # Magnetic rotation
    t = q * B * dt / (2 * m * gamma_minus)
    s = 2 * t / (1 + np.dot(t, t))
    u_prime = u_minus + np.cross(u_minus, t)
    u_plus = u_minus + np.cross(u_prime, s)
    
    # Half electric push
    u_new = u_plus + (q * E / m) * (dt / 2)
    gamma_new = np.sqrt(1 + np.dot(u_new, u_new) / c**2)
    
    # Position update
    x_new = x + (u_new / gamma_new) * dt
    
    return x_new, u_new, gamma_new
```

## **Appendix B. Gravitational Afterimage**

---

### **B.1 Definition**

When a mass ceases to exist — whether by annihilation, decay, or passage through an event horizon — the gravitational influence of that mass does not instantaneously vanish at distant points. The termination signal propagates outward at _c_. Until that signal arrives at a distance _r_, observers at that distance continue to experience the gravitational field of a mass that no longer exists.

This is not a hypothesis. It is a direct consequence of the finite propagation speed of gravity, applied to the case of a time-varying mass. It is the gravitational analog of the well-known fact that we observe stars as they were years or centuries ago, not as they are now — including stars that no longer exist.

**Definition:** The _gravitational afterimage_ of a mass M_M_ that ceases to exist at time t0_t_0​ is the gravitational field observed at distance r_r_ during the interval:

t0<t<t0+rc_t_0​<_t_<_t_0​+_cr_​

**Unicode:** Duration of afterimage = r / c

**LaTeX:**

Δtafterimage(r)=rcΔ_tafterimage_​(_r_)=_cr_​

**Mathematica:**

```
deltaT[r_] := r/c
```

**Python:**

```
def afterimage_duration(r, c):
    return r / c
```

For a mass at the galactic center, the afterimage persists at the outer disk (r≈15_r_≈15 kpc) for:

Δt=15,kpc306.4,kpc/Myr≈49,000,yearsΔ_t_=306.4,kpc/Myr15,kpc​≈49,000,years

---

### **B.2 Afterimage Accumulation**

If mass is being continuously created and destroyed near the galactic center — as proposed in Claim II — and if the destruction timescale Tann_Tann_​ is short compared to the light-travel time across the disk:

Tann≪rc_Tann_​≪_cr_​

then afterimages accumulate. At any given moment, the gravitational field at radius _r_ includes contributions from masses that no longer exist, generated at all times in the interval [t−r/c,,t][_t_−_r_/_c_,,_t_].

The effective “ghost” mass seen at radius _r_ is:

Mghost(r)=∫t−r/ctM˙ann(t′),dt′_Mghost_​(_r_)=∫_t_−_r_/_ct_​_M_˙_ann_​(_t_′),_dt_′

For a constant annihilation rate M˙ann_M_˙_ann_​:

Mghost(r)=M˙ann⋅rc_Mghost_​(_r_)=_M_˙_ann_​⋅_cr_​

This is a mass that grows linearly with distance from the source — precisely the behavior needed to explain flat rotation curves. A flat rotation curve requires Menc(r)∝r_Menc_​(_r_)∝_r_ (since vc2=GMenc/r_vc_2​=_GMenc_​/_r_ = constant implies Menc∝r_Menc_​∝_r_). The ghost mass formula gives exactly this dependence if M˙ann_M_˙_ann_​ is roughly constant.

**LaTeX:**

Mghost(r)=M˙ann⋅rc,vc2=GMghostr=GM˙annc=const_Mghost_​(_r_)=_M_˙_ann_​⋅_cr_​,_vc_2​=_rGMghost_​​=_cGM_˙_ann_​​=const

**Mathematica:**

```
Mghost[r_] := Mdotann * r/c
vc2ghost[r_] := G * Mghost[r] / r  (* = G*Mdotann/c, independent of r *)
```

**Python:**

```
def ghost_mass(r, Mdot_ann, c):
    return Mdot_ann * r / c

def vc_ghost_squared(Mdot_ann, G, c):
    return G * Mdot_ann / c  # constant, independent of r
```

This is a remarkable structural result: **if pair production and annihilation near the galactic center proceed at a roughly constant rate, the gravitational afterimage of the annihilated mass produces a flat rotation curve at all radii, with no additional free parameters beyond M˙ann**_**M**_**˙**_**ann**_**​.**

This does not prove that this is the mechanism. The value of M˙ann_M_˙_ann_​ required to explain observed rotation curve anomalies is not computed here. It is identified as a quantitative target for future work.

---

### **B.3 Spatial Distribution of Ghost Mass**

The spatial distribution of ghost mass is not uniform. It is determined by the geometry of the pair production and annihilation region. If production is localized to within a radius r0_r_0​ of the galactic center, then:

- At r<r0_r_<_r_0​: contribution from all annihilation events within the light cone
    
- At r>r0_r_>_r_0​: ghost mass is dominated by events near the center, giving the Mghost∝r_Mghost_​∝_r_ scaling above
    

The expected distribution — concentrated toward the galactic center but extending to large radii — is qualitatively similar to the observed dark matter halo profile (NFW profile: Navarro, Frenk & White, 1997):

ρNFW(r)=ρ0(r/rs)(1+r/rs)2_ρNFW_​(_r_)=(_r_/_rs_​)(1+_r_/_rs_​)2_ρ_0​​

A quantitative comparison between the predicted ghost mass distribution and the NFW profile is identified as a key verification step.

**LaTeX — NFW profile:**

ρNFW(r)=ρ0(r/rs)(1+r/rs)2_ρNFW_​(_r_)=(_r_/_rs_​)(1+_r_/_rs_​)2_ρ_0​​

**Mathematica:**

```
rhoNFW[r_] := rho0 / ((r/rs)*(1 + r/rs)^2)
```

**Python:**

```
def rho_NFW(r, rho0, rs):
    return rho0 / ((r/rs) * (1 + r/rs)**2)
```

## **Appendix C. Magnetic Pair Production and Annihilation Suppression**

---

### **C.1 Cyclotron Radius and Spatial Confinement**

A relativistic charged particle moving perpendicular to a magnetic field executes circular motion with cyclotron (Larmor) radius:

rL=p⊥∣q∣B=γmv⊥∣q∣B_rL_​=∣_q_∣_Bp_⊥​​=∣_q_∣_Bγmv_⊥​​

where p⊥=γmv⊥_p_⊥​=_γmv_⊥​ is the component of relativistic momentum perpendicular to **B**.

**LaTeX:**

rL=γmv⊥∣q∣B_rL_​=∣_q_∣_Bγmv_⊥​​

**Mathematica:**

```
rL[gamma_, m_, vperp_, q_, B_] := gamma*m*vperp / (Abs[q]*B)
```

**Python:**

```
def larmor_radius(gamma, m, v_perp, q, B):
    return gamma * m * abs(v_perp) / (abs(q) * B)
```

As B→Bc_B_→_Bc_​ (Schwinger field), rL→rquantum=ℏ/(mec)_rL_​→_rquantum_​=ℏ/(_me_​_c_) (reduced Compton wavelength ≈3.86×10−13≈3.86×10−13 m). In this limit, the particle cannot propagate more than a Compton wavelength from its production point before the magnetic force returns it. Pair annihilation requires spatial overlap between the particle and antiparticle wave functions. If both are confined to a Compton-scale region but in opposite gyration orbits, the overlap integral determines the annihilation rate. In the extreme field limit, this overlap is suppressed by the separation of the gyration centers.

### **C.2 Gravitational Drift and Systematic Separation**

In a combined gravitational field **g** and magnetic field **B**, the drift velocity of a charged particle is:

vg=m,g×B∣q∣B2**v**_g_​=∣_q_∣_B_2_m_,**g**×**B**​

The sign of q_q_ reverses the direction of drift. A particle-antiparticle pair produced at the same point in a region with both **g** and **B** will drift in opposite directions at the same speed.

For a radial gravitational field g=−gr^**g**=−_gr_^ and an azimuthal magnetic field B=Bϕ^**B**=_Bϕ_^​ (a simplified model of the black hole magnetosphere):

vg(±)=±mg∣e∣B,(r^×ϕ^)=±mg∣e∣B,z^**v**_g_(±)​=±∣_e_∣_Bmg_​,(_r_^×_ϕ_^​)=±∣_e_∣_Bmg_​,_z_^

The pair separates vertically (along the rotation axis), with the particle moving toward one pole and the antiparticle toward the other. This is the mechanism proposed for concentration of antiparticles in the region opposite the accretion disk.

**LaTeX:**

vg=m,g×B∣q∣B2,vg(±)=±mg∣e∣Bz^**v**_g_​=∣_q_∣_B_2_m_,**g**×**B**​,**v**_g_(±)​=±∣_e_∣_Bmg_​_z_^

**Mathematica:**

```
vDrift[m_, g_, q_, B_] := m * Cross[g, B] / (Abs[q] * B^2)
```

**Python:**

```
import numpy as np
def grav_drift(m, g_vec, q, B_vec):
    B_sq = np.dot(B_vec, B_vec)
    return m * np.cross(g_vec, B_vec) / (abs(q) * B_sq)
```

---

### **C.3 Experimental Confirmation in Yang-Mills v3.2**

The gravitational drift separation is directly confirmed in the Yang-Mills Collider v3.2 simulation:

**Condition 1 (B ≠ 0, BH mass > 0):** Particle-antiparticle pairs are produced at the BH. Within a few simulation steps, the two components separate spatially. The antiparticle component drifts to the hemisphere opposite the accretion disk. Displaced vertex measurements confirm finite propagation distances before decay.

**Condition 2 (B = 0, BH mass > 0):** With the magnetic field zeroed, both components are immediately accreted by the black hole. No spatial separation occurs. No displaced vertex structure is observed.

The control experiment (Condition 2) establishes that the separation in Condition 1 is attributable to the magnetic field, not to asymmetries in the gravitational field alone.

---

### **C.4 Annihilation Rate as a Function of Separation**

The electron-positron annihilation rate per pair is:

Γann=n⋅σann⋅vrelΓ_ann_​=_n_⋅_σann_​⋅_vrel_​

where n_n_ is the local number density of the opposite-sign particle, σann_σann_​ is the annihilation cross-section, and vrel_vrel_​ is the relative velocity.

For non-relativistic e+e−_e_+_e_−:

σann=πre2β(1−β2)[2+41−β2−2β2(1−β2)3/2]≈πre2β(β≪1)_σann_​=_βπre_2​​(1−_β_2)[2+1−_β_24​−(1−_β_2)3/22_β_2​]≈_βπre_2​​(_β_≪1)

where re=e2/(mec2)≈2.82×10−13_re_​=_e_2/(_me_​_c_2)≈2.82×10−13 cm is the classical electron radius.

Spatial separation reduces n_n_ exponentially with separation distance d_d_. If the pair production region has radius r0_r_0​ and the gravitational drift moves the antiparticle to a new region of size r0_r_0​, then:

noverlap∝exp⁡!(−d22r02)_noverlap_​∝exp!(−2_r_02​_d_2​)

For d≫r0_d_≫_r_0​ (strong magnetic field, large separation), Γann→0Γ_ann_​→0.

**LaTeX:**

Γann≈πre2cβ⋅noverlap,noverlap∝e−d2/(2r02)Γ_ann_​≈_βπre_2​_c_​⋅_noverlap_​,_noverlap_​∝_e_−_d_2/(2_r_02​)

**Mathematica:**

```
re = 2.818e-13;  (* classical electron radius, cm *)
sigmaAnn[beta_] := Pi * re^2 / beta
nOverlap[d_, r0_] := Exp[-d^2 / (2*r0^2)]
GammaAnn[beta_, d_, r0_, n0_] := n0 * nOverlap[d,r0] * sigmaAnn[beta] * beta*c
```

**Python:**

```
import numpy as np
re = 2.818e-15   # classical electron radius, m

def sigma_annihilation(beta):
    return np.pi * re**2 / beta

def annihilation_rate(beta, d, r0, n0, c):
    n_overlap = n0 * np.exp(-d**2 / (2*r0**2))
    return n_overlap * sigma_annihilation(beta) * beta * c
```

## **Appendix D. CPT Symmetry and the Verification Gap**

---

### **D.1 The Standard Statement**

CPT symmetry — the combined invariance of physical laws under charge conjugation (C), parity reversal (P), and time reversal (T) — is a theorem of relativistic quantum field theory (Lüders, 1954; Pauli, 1955). It holds in any local, Lorentz-invariant quantum field theory. No violation has been observed in any laboratory experiment.

ALPHA Collaboration (CERN) has measured the spectrum of antihydrogen and confirmed that the 1S-2S transition frequency of Hˉ_H_ˉ agrees with that of H to within 2×10−122×10−12 relative precision (Ahmadi et al., 2018). This is among the most precise tests of CPT symmetry.

---

### **D.2 The Conditions Under Which CPT Has Been Tested**

Every precision CPT test conducted to date shares the following environmental conditions:

ParameterCERN experimental conditionsBlack hole environmentVacuum quality10−1310−13 torr (best achievable)<10−20<10−20 torr (interstellar)Gravitational field∼10∼10 m/s² (Earth surface)∼1012∼1012 m/s² (near horizon)Magnetic field≲104≲104 Gauss (trap field)∼106∼106–10141014 Gauss (estimate)Spacetime curvatureEssentially flatExtremeEnergy scale≲≲ GeVArbitrary

The combination of all four extreme conditions simultaneously — ultra-high vacuum, extreme gravity, extreme magnetic field, and extreme spacetime curvature — has never been realized in a laboratory setting. CPT has not been tested in this regime.

This is not a claim that CPT is violated in strong-field environments. It is an observation about the boundary of empirical knowledge.

---

### **D.3 Theoretical Context**

CPT invariance is a consequence of the CPT theorem, which requires:

1. Lorentz invariance
    
2. Locality (interactions at a point)
    
3. The spin-statistics theorem
    

In the vicinity of a black hole horizon, conditions (1) and (2) are subject to known theoretical challenges:

- The information paradox (Hawking, 1976) involves apparent non-unitarity, which may conflict with the assumptions underlying the CPT theorem in the full quantum gravity context.
    
- Loop quantum gravity and string theory predict Lorentz-violating corrections at the Planck scale.
    
- The combination of extreme curvature and strong electromagnetic fields constitutes a regime where quantum gravity effects may not be negligible.
    

The present document makes no claim about CPT violation. It notes that the Yang-Mills v3.2 simulation operates in a formally CPT-symmetric framework (the Boris integrator preserves time-reversal symmetry, and particles and antiparticles are treated symmetrically up to charge sign). The observed asymmetric separation is a consequence of the charge-dependent drift, not a CPT violation.

However, the broader question — whether the behavior of antimatter in the extreme black hole environment is fully characterized by laboratory CPT tests — is identified as a legitimate open question in the literature (Kostelecký & Russell, 2011; CPT and Lorentz Symmetry series).

---

### **D.4 Antihydrogen in Space: The Relevant Experiment That Has Not Been Done**

The ALPHA-g experiment (2023) has measured the free-fall acceleration of antihydrogen in Earth’s gravitational field and confirmed gHˉ=gH_gH_ˉ​=_gH_​ to within the experimental uncertainty (ALPHA Collaboration, 2023). This is the first direct test of the gravitational interaction of antimatter.

The relevant open question is not the sign of gravitational attraction of antimatter (confirmed to be attractive, same as matter) but the behavior of antimatter in the combined regime of extreme magnetic confinement and extreme spacetime curvature. This regime is not accessible to current experiments and is not addressed by any existing measurement.

## **1. What Is Currently Implemented**

### **1.1 Newtonian Gravity**

The simplest form of gravity:

```
F = G * M * m / r^2
```

In code, log-corrected to match scene scale:

```
gAcc = log10(BH_MASS + 1) * 120.0 / (r^2 + 1.0)
dp/dt = -gAcc * (r_hat) * dt
```

Rather than curving spacetime, this approach directly adds force to momentum.  
  
It is Newtonian — yet all particles, regardless of mass or charge, are attracted equally.  
  
This effectively reproduces the **Equivalence Principle**.

---

### **1.2 Event Horizon Approximation**

The event horizon radius of a Kerr black hole:

```
r_+ = M * (1 + sqrt(1 - a*^2))
```

where a* = a/M is the dimensionless spin parameter (0 to 1).

Code approximation:

```
Rs_base = max(0.8, log10(BH_MASS + 1) * 0.6)
Rs = Rs_base * (1 + sqrt(max(0, 1 - BH_SPIN^2))) * 0.5
if r < Rs * 2 : absorbed = true
```

Reflects the fact that the horizon shrinks to its minimum when spin is maximal (a* = 1).

### **1.3 Frame Dragging — Lense-Thirring Approximation**

A defining effect of the Kerr metric. A rotating mass drags the surrounding spacetime along with it.

Exact expression:

```
Ω_LT ~ 2 * G * J / (c^2 * r^3)
```

where J = angular momentum.

Code approximation (simplified as a tangential force):

```
r_xz = sqrt(dx^2 + dz^2)
fdAcc = BH_SPIN * log10(BH_MASS+1) * 55.0 / (r^3 + 1.0)
tx = -dz / r_xz   (tangential unit vector, Y-axis rotation)
tz =  dx / r_xz
dp_x/dt += fdAcc * tx * dt
dp_z/dt += fdAcc * tz * dt
```

A tangential acceleration is used instead of full tensor computation.  
  
Despite this, spiral infall and the Penrose process emerge as outcomes.

---

### **1.4 Ergosphere**

A region unique to Kerr black holes. Outside the event horizon, yet spacetime itself rotates.  
  
Remaining stationary here is impossible — everything is forced to co-rotate with the black hole.

Ergosphere radius at the equator:

```
r_ergo = 2M  (at a* = 1, equatorial plane)
r_ergo = M * (1 + sqrt(1 - a*^2 * cos^2(theta)))  (general)
```

Code approximation:

```
r_ergo_approx = Rs_base * 2.0
if r < r_ergo and BH_SPIN > 0.1:
    ergoBoost = BH_SPIN * logMass * 80.0 / (r^2 + 1.0)
    additional tangential acceleration applied
```

Visualized as a purple wireframe when spin > 0.15.

## **2. Phenomena That Emerged Without Design**

### **2.1 Equivalence Principle**

All particles — regardless of charge, mass, or type — are absorbed without exception.  
  
Simply adding Newtonian gravity produced the Equivalence Principle as an outcome.

### **2.2 Accretion Disk Formation**

Particles spawned with random momenta spontaneously aggregate into a disk structure.  
  
A consequence of angular momentum conservation. Not designed — yet it appeared.

### **2.3 Penrose Process**

Under strong magnetic field + spin conditions, particles near the ergosphere  
  
were observed to escape. Mathematically predicted by Roger Penrose in 1969;  
  
observationally confirmed in 2021.

```
E_escape = E_particle + Ω_H * L
```

This simulator did not intentionally implement this.  
  
It emerged spontaneously from the interaction of  
  
Newtonian gravity + frame-dragging approximation + Boris magnetic field integration.

## **3. What Was Left Out — and Why**

### **3.1 Full Kerr Metric**

The exact description of spacetime:

```
ds^2 = -(1 - r_s*r/Sigma)*c^2*dt^2
       - (2*r_s*r*a*sin^2(theta)/Sigma)*c*dt*dphi
       + (Sigma/Delta)*dr^2
       + Sigma*d(theta)^2
       + (r^2 + a^2 + r_s*r*a^2*sin^2(theta)/Sigma)*sin^2(theta)*dphi^2

Sigma = r^2 + a^2*cos^2(theta)
Delta = r^2 - r_s*r + a^2
```

Particle trajectories must follow the geodesic equation:

```
d^2 x^mu / d lambda^2 + Gamma^mu_alpha_beta * (dx^alpha/dlambda) * (dx^beta/dlambda) = 0
```

Computing Christoffel symbols Γ every frame is not feasible in real-time in a browser.  
  
**Not implemented: technical constraints.**

### **3.2 Hawking Radiation**

The emission of energy from a black hole via quantum effects:

```
T_H = hbar * c^3 / (8 * pi * G * M * k_B)
```

Implementation would require quantum field theory on curved spacetime (Bogoliubov transformations) —  
  
a fundamentally different layer from the current engine architecture.  
  
**Not implemented: technical and theoretical constraints.**

](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!kCdv!,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fab971392-202b-4cfc-9483-0b503badcb2f_1069x1114.png)

### **3.3 Tidal Forces / Spaghettification**

```
dF_tidal ~ 2 * G * M * m * dr / r^3
```

The gravitational gradient arising from distance differences — the effect that stretches objects.  
  
Not applicable in the current architecture, which treats each particle as a single point mass.  
  
**Not implemented: technical constraints.**

### **3.4 Gravitational Waves**

Upon merger of two massive bodies:

```
h ~ G * M * v^2 / (c^4 * r)
```

Oscillations in spacetime itself. Structurally impossible here,  
  
as the background spacetime is fixed as flat (Minkowski).  
  
**Not implemented: technical constraints.**

## **4. What Was Left Out — As a Physical Choice**

The omissions above are partly due to technical limitations —  
  
but they are equally **intentional choices**.

Implementing full GR would actually cost something:

> How do small masses move around a large gravitational body —  
>   
> seeing that simplest question through the simplest possible means.

When Kepler observed planetary orbits,  
  
he did not know why they were ellipses — but he saw that they were ellipses first.

This simulator is the same.  
  
Rather than why the equations produce those orbits —  
  
the goal is to see **whether those orbits actually appear**.

Even with Newtonian gravity being wrong, the Penrose process emerged.  
  
That is the honesty and value of this approach.

# LHC Simulation: Physics Formulas and Explanations

[](https://substack.com/@seoulinside)

[B. Sun | Seoul Inside](https://substack.com/@seoulinside)
